# 28. Comprehensive Test Suite Inventory & Isolation Map

**Document Version:** 1.48.0
**Date:** 2026-09-25
**Lane:** Testing Lane (Antigravity-6)  
**Scope:** `du-rework` entire test topology (businesses, services, packages, tests)  
**Isolation Standard:** MM-13 Per-Run Test Isolation Framework (`tests/isolation/namespace.ts`)

---

## 1. Executive Summary

A comprehensive inventory of all test suites across `du-rework` was conducted to establish a deterministic testing topology and enforce strict isolation between offline unit tests and live infrastructure-dependent integration tests.

### Key Metrics (historical snapshot; not a current count)

> Sections 1–7 retain the previously reconciled inventory snapshot. They are not a current on-disk suite total. Section 8 adds named Cycle 96–139 evidence without summing overlapping focused runs or claiming that this audit-note update reran them.

- **Total Test Suites:** 106 suites (81 + 82 điều chỉnh C1 → cộng thêm 2 BROWSER, W43-Q6)
- **OFFLINE Suites (Zero Infra Required):** 82 suites (77.4%)
- **LIVE_INFRA Suites (PostgreSQL :5433 and/or Redis :6380):** 22 suites (20.8%)
  - Pure Redis (:6380): 1 suite (`bullmq-smoke.test.ts`)
  - Pure PostgreSQL (:5433): 1 suite (`migrations.test.ts`)
  - PostgreSQL (:5433) + Redis (:6380): 20 suites
- **BROWSER Suites (Playwright chromium-only, offline, zero DB/Redis — W43-Q6):** 2 suites (`tests/browser/tests/sections.spec.ts`, `tests/browser/tests/interactions.spec.ts`)

---

## 2. Infrastructure Gating & Isolation Protocol

### 2.1 The Two-Phase Testing Model
1. **OFFLINE Phase (Zero Infrastructure):**
   - Pure unit, contract, schema, AST parsing, and in-memory mock suites.
   - Requires NO Docker containers, NO ports, NO locks, NO database credentials.
   - Completely safe to execute concurrently across multiple development lanes without contention.
2. **LIVE_INFRA Phase (Gated by Shared DB Window):**
   - Requires PostgreSQL on `127.0.0.1:5433` and/or Redis on `127.0.0.1:6380`.
   - **Gating Rule:** Must NEVER execute until the shared DB window is officially released by the platform lane (Claude Code) in `du-rework/coordination/reports/claude.md` with literal `'DB RELEASED'`.
   - **Claim Rule:** The testing lane claims the window in writing via `reports/antigravity-6.md` with `DB CLAIMED`.
   - **Isolation Rule (MM-13):** Every live suite must run with per-run schema isolation (`TEST_RUN_ID=<run_id>`, `REDIS_DB_INDEX=<index>`), preventing global `TRUNCATE` collisions and table locking contention.
   - **Release Rule:** The testing lane immediately writes `DB RELEASED` when execution concludes.

## 5. Execution Guide: Unified Batch Runner

The testing runner `du-rework/tests/isolation/concurrent-runner.ps1` has been expanded to provide a single, unified execution interface ("chạy tập dưới một nhãn") with built-in MM-13 isolation harness support.

### 5.1 Running the Offline Suite Batch (Zero DB / Safe)
```powershell
# From repository root or du-rework directory:
.\du-rework\tests\isolation\concurrent-runner.ps1 -Mode Batch -Category Offline
```
- Executes all 81 offline suites.
- Does NOT require PostgreSQL or Redis.
- Safe to run at any time regardless of DB window ownership.

### 5.2 Running the Live Suite Batch (Gated & Isolated)
```powershell
# Prerequisite: Claude Code has written "DB RELEASED" in reports/claude.md
# Testing lane has recorded "DB CLAIMED" in reports/antigravity-6.md
.\du-rework\tests\isolation\concurrent-runner.ps1 -Mode Batch -Category Live
```
- Allocates dynamic MM-13 isolation sandboxes (`TEST_RUN_ID`, `REDIS_DB_INDEX`, schema `du_test_<runId>`).
- Executes all 23 live suites sequentially without cross-run data corruption or table locks.
- Collects and prints literal `'Tests: N passed, M total'` per suite.

### 5.3 Running Concurrent Isolation Verification
```powershell
.\du-rework\tests\isolation\concurrent-runner.ps1 -Mode Concurrent -TargetSuite "tests/runtime.test.ts" -OffsetSeconds 5
```
- Proves that two overlapping test runs execute concurrently with zero cross-run cleanup and zero contention.

## 6. W43-Q6 Addendum — Offline Re-Run 07:14–07:19 + 5 OFFLINE Suites mới (Qwen-2)

### 6.1 Offline regression batch (concurrent-runner.ps1 -Category Offline, literal runner output)
- **82 suites = 81 PASS + 1 FAIL thoáng qua** | **Tests Passed Sum: 1469 | Tests Total Sum: 1525**.
- FAIL duy nhất: `services/orchestrator/tests/admin-shell-server.test.ts` — `Tests: 2 failed, 54 passed, 56 total`, Exit 1 (5.05s) **chỉ trong khối batch load toàn bộ**.

No suite was deleted, renamed or reclassified, and no test total anywhere in this document was adjusted to match a receipt. **The three traps a re-counter will hit here, stated before they are hit again.** First, `it.each([...OPERATIONS_LIST_SORT_VALUES])` reads as one line and expands to **six** rows; there are five such tables in the binding suite and one in the pagination suite, so a line-based count reports 21 where the real figure is 54. Second, two tables are named constants — `combos` (7 rows) and `malformed` (14 rows) — and a scan for `{ label:` counts them **only if it skips the declaration line**, because the type annotation `const combos: { label: string; … }[]` also contains `{ label:`. Counting naively gives 8 and 15, a total of 56. Third, the binding suite has **6** `describe` blocks, not 5: the Mục 15.4 prose says "54 test / 5 describe", and the sixth is the companion-finding block at line 368, which was added for the `WHERE`-fold defect. The test count is unaffected; the describe count in the receipt prose is one low.

**No gate moved and no row was ticked.** `G-ADMIN-OPS` remains NO-GO, because a green base-route suite does not finish the list surface: the other three Admin lists still have no sort allowlist, `updated_at` and `deadline_at` still have no index, the audit list still lacks time/actor/resource filters, and the seeded C1-C5 browser acceptance has not run. The four gates are unchanged: `G-ADMIN-OPS` NO-GO, `G-SEC` NO-GO, `G-DATA` NO-GO, `G6` NO-GO.

### 8.22 D-EVID-A27 — Turn 180 becomes the current audit pointer, and the cursor/sort prose moves onto the wire policy read from source

| **The operations-list wire policy, re-read from source rather than quoted from the packet** | [Turn 180 evidence row](../coordination/reports/review.md#L956) and its [work order](../coordination/reports/review.md#L975); the source symbols are named in the cell to the right | Four facts, all read out of `services/orchestrator/src/server.ts` and `packages/contracts/src/public-api.ts` this cycle. (1) The cursor payload is **four slots at most**, `base64url("<ISO>\|<uuid>\|<field>:<direction>[\|p]")`, assembled by `encodeOperationsListCursor` from the instant, the id and `formatOperationsListSort(sort)`, with a `\|p` suffix for a backward walk. (2) `decodeOperationsListCursor` splits the payload on `\|`, pops a trailing `p` **as its own part** (it is not a legal `field:direction`, so the two shapes cannot be confused) and parses what remains through `parseOperationsListSort`; it accepts 2 to 4 parts only, and a two-slot token reads as the default ordering. (3) `parseOperationsListQuery` throws **422 `INVALID_SCHEMA`** when `decoded.sort.field !== sort.field \|\| decoded.sort.direction !== sort.direction`, and throws a second 422 when the token is malformed. (4) The allow-list is `OPERATIONS_LIST_SORT_FIELDS` (`created_at`, `updated_at`, `deadline_at`) x `OPERATIONS_LIST_SORT_DIRECTIONS` (`asc`, `desc`) = **exactly six** wire values, with `LIST_CURSOR_MAX_LEN` = 128. | **A documentation sync, at a level the Reviewer already set and this cycle did not raise.** Turn 180 adjudicates T140-A1 as **IMPLEMENTED, offline VERIFIED, live acceptance OPEN**: no live receipt covers a cross-sort 422, a legacy token, a six-sort walk or a query plan on the new sorts, and the live keyset suite still queries only `created_at`. Reading the policy out of source raises nothing, because the packet that closed the wire behaviour is not the packet that accepts it. One behaviour note worth keeping: `bindOperationsCursor` is **unchanged** — it still compares `(sortKeySql, id)` against `($N::timestamptz, $M::uuid)`; what changed is that a mismatched cursor is refused *before* it is ever bound, so the row-value predicate was never the fix. |
| **Three prose sites corrected — the exact lines Turn 180 named** | [`docs/06-public-api.md`](../docs/06-public-api.md#L80), [`docs/20-openapi-descriptions.md`](../docs/20-openapi-descriptions.md#L39) and [`docs/admin-ops-monitoring-cost.md`](../docs/admin-ops-monitoring-cost.md#L21) | Turn 180 cited `docs/06-public-api.md:92-94`, `docs/20-openapi-descriptions.md:42` and `docs/admin-ops-monitoring-cost.md:21` as still describing behaviour the source contradicts. All three carried a **present-tense** claim of the old policy: the two-slot token `base64url("<ISO>\|<uuid>")`; the paragraph headed **MISMATCH T140-A1 — OPEN**, which said the token does not carry the sort identity; and the Admin status cell *"không có cursor/filter/sort server-side"*. The present tense was rewritten at every site, while the finding history was kept in both former MISMATCH blocks and explicitly not restated as acceptance. | **The old statements are preserved as history rather than deleted, because the Reviewer asked for exactly that split.** Turn 170 ordered the Docs/plan step to update stale current-state cursor/Admin prose and the current-review pointer from confirmed source, and to preserve historical A15/Δ23 statements as exact-scope history without rewriting them as acceptance for newer behaviour. Two craft points are worth recording. First, the third site is a **markdown table cell**, and the correct payload notation carries three `\|` characters — written unescaped they split one row into seven cells and silently broke the table. A generic row counter under-reports this leg by ten. |

Materialized artifact consistency is unchecked on replay and on insert conflict. The route orders `deadline_at` through `COALESCE` while migration 0018 indexes the **bare** column, so the planner may still sort. T180-D3 as written — a source URL can stay parked with no operator signal when the storage backend is PostgreSQL, because the consumer is created only for S3 while the dispatcher always excludes `gate='ingestion'` — **is now admission-rejected: `sourceUrl` on a non-S3 backend returns 422 `UNSUPPORTED_STORAGE_BACKEND` (`services/orchestrator/src/modules/operations/submission.ts:113-122`, `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts` `PublicErrorCodes`) with zero DB writes; see [docs/06-public-api.md §Submission](../docs/06-public-api.md) and §Errors. | **T180-D1/D2/A1 untouched — each names a product owner.** T180-A1’s index/expression mismatch was assigned to Admin/Tester for live EXPLAIN with a follow-on migration, not an edit to applied 0018. **T180-D3’s documentation surface is now synced (T200-D1)**: the parked-row prose is preserved as history in this cell and annotated with the admission-time 422 that supersedes it; the fix itself is product-owned and offline VERIFIED, not live ACCEPTED. |

**The test figure for this cycle is a declaration count, and the executed count is higher — the same trap §12.23 records, pointing the other way from the COST 122.** The three offline suites behind the cursor binding are `operations-list-cursor-sort-binding.test.ts` (488 lines, 16 `it(` plus 4 `test(`, **5 `.each` blocks**), `admin-operations-sort-wiring.test.ts` (515 lines, 27 `it(`, **3 `.each` blocks**) and `admin-keyset-explain.test.ts` (359 lines, 6 `it(` plus 2 `test(`). Literal declarations total **55**; the executed count is higher because 8 declarations sit inside tables. The [Turn 180 row](../coordination/reports/review.md#L956) records **190/190** for T-32 across three offline sort suites, and that is the executed figure. **This cycle did not derive 190 and does not restate it as its own measurement** — only 55 is reproducible from the tree, and quoting 190 as though it had counted the tests would be asserting something it never checked. The `admin-keyset-explain` suite also self-skips offline by construction (`const liveDescribe = LIVE ? describe : describe.skip`), which is a gate and not a defect, so its 8 declarations are not an offline green.

**Link check, and a scope gap that has now recurred on a second file.** The scoped check is **S0 881 targets / 564 anchors / BROKEN 0**, **S1 890 / 573 / BROKEN 0**, and a new **A27 scope 872 / 521 / BROKEN 0** covering the four files this cycle edited plus `docs/28`, `docs/35` and this report. The whole-tree pass still reports exactly the **two** pre-existing breaks in other lanes’s files (`coordination/reports/antigravity-6.md:8952` and `coordination/requests/antigravity.md:10`) and one non-UTF-8 file (`coordination/reports/tester3.md`). The gap: cycle 19 found `tasks/README.md` sat in **neither** S0 nor S1, so the most invasively edited file ran zero link checks; A27 was added for it, and `docs/admin-ops-monitoring-cost.md` is in **neither** S0, S1 nor A26, so the file this cycle rewrote most heavily had the same blind spot. The lesson generalises — **a per-cycle scope derived from what was edited last cycle is not coverage**, and the standing scopes should be widened rather than added to.

**No gate moved, no row was ticked, and no acceptance level rose.** The four gates stand exactly as Turn 180 left them: `G-ADMIN-OPS` NO-GO, `G-SEC` NO-GO, `G-DATA` NO-GO, `G6` NO-GO. Nothing in §8.22 should be quoted as evidence that the cursor work moved a release gate; T140-A1 remains one live multi-tenant pagination run with a stable final diff away from a Reviewer decision, and only the Reviewer can make that decision. The sync performed here is the Docs/plan half of Turn 180’s own order, and that order is the last item on it.

It is now a member of ``PublicErrorCodes``, and ``docs/06`` publishes the backend condition beside ``sourceUrl`` and in the status-code list. The change is offline-verified by its owner; this row records that the *documentation surface* now matches the code. |

Both pass a ``1 <= n <= lineCount`` test, so ``BROKEN=0`` cannot see this class — the same failure A28 had just diagnosed and fixed for its own ``#L1660``. **The Reviewer asked for an instrument, not only a re-point, and the instrument is the part that mattered:** an anchor-content assertion now runs with the link check. | The assertion fires only when the **link text itself** carries a finding ID, because that is the precise claim. Naming a finding elsewhere in the same prose line is not a claim about the anchor, and asserting on it produced 22 false positives that the tighter rule removed. On its first clean run it immediately caught a second instance, in the next row. |
| **A second off-by-one, found by the new instrument rather than by the link check** | [T200-D1 at line 1045](../coordination/reports/review.md#L1045) | The T200-D1 sync cited ``#L1044`` for T200-D1 in both this file and ``docs/35``. Line 1044 is **T200-P2**; T200-D1 is at **1045**. One line out, still in range, invisible to ``BROKEN=0``, and fixed in both files this cycle. This is the first defect the new assertion has caught, which is the only evidence yet that it earns its running cost. | One defect found is not a clean bill of health. The assertion covers S0/S1 and links whose text names a finding, so a stale anchor pointing at a line with no finding ID is still uncaught. |
| **A third defect the A28 gate could not have caught — the SQL parameter form, one dollar instead of two** | ``bindOperationsCursor`` in ``services/orchestrator/src/server.ts`` | ``docs/28:702`` carried ``($n::timestamptz, $n::uuid)`` — one parameter cast to two incompatible types. A28 searched for the ``$$n`` corruption and fixed four sites; this line uses a single ``$n``, so it survived that search while asserting something impossible. The route emits **two distinct positions**, which is why the published form is ``($N::timestamptz, $M::uuid)``. | Cited by symbol rather than line number, per the fleet convention T200-P2 re-affirmed: the file this describes was under another lane active edit all cycle and its line numbers moved underneath the citation. |
| **Standing scope widened, per-cycle scopes retired** | [tasks/README.md:3](../tasks/README.md#L3), now naming Turn 200 at ``review.md#L1018`` | S0 grows to 8 files by adding ``tasks/README.md`` and ``docs/admin-ops-monitoring-cost.md``; S1 inherits them and reaches 11. Those are the two files most heavily edited across recent cycles that had run under no standing scope at all. No A2x scope is minted this cycle. | Scope is coverage, not permission. Widening the checker widens no write permission and moves no gate. |

**The pointer moved, and a pointer is not an adjudication.** ``tasks/README.md:3`` named Turn 190 at ``review.md#L977`` and now names Turn 200 at ``#L1018``, verified to be that heading. The 52 unaccepted release-scope rows were re-derived leg by leg again and are unchanged at 14 P0–P8 + 16 SEC + 8 Admin UX + 10 DATA/LOG/DEP + 4 COST = 52. Turn 200 itself records that no task row changed in that audit, and the 52 stays a task-row inventory, not a readiness percentage.

The CONTENT gate holds with **0** ``$$`` and **0** placeholder tokens inside S0 and S1. ``tsc --noEmit`` on the contracts package exits 0, so the enum addition is not a type error.

### 8.30 D-EVID-A27 — RESULT-WIRE-01: contract 200 da freeze, va docs/06 con ghi 302 da duoc sua

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **Muc nay dong mot contract** chu khong phai them mot so test. `GET /artifacts/{id}/download` **khong con 302** — catalog `docs/06-public-api.md` da doi sang **200 raw bytes / 200 JSON wrapper**; `GET /operations/{id}/result` da duoc chot la **200 strict v1** o ca plain va encrypted mode.

| Bang chung (doc truc tiep) | Noi dung |
|---|---|
| [tester.md#L8245](../coordination/reports/tester.md#L8245) `RESULT-WIRE-01` (04:07:35 +07) | `@du/contracts` build **0**; test **19 suites / 427 tests** **0**; `@du/orchestrator` `tests/delivery-encryption.test.ts` **22/22** **0**; `tsc --noEmit` sach **0** ca hai package |
| Fixture trong cung receipt | validate schema, noi artifact reference cua result **da giai ma**, roi **giai ma ben ngoai va so byte** payload that |

**Contract da freeze, dung bang chung doc duoc:**

- `GET /api/v1/operations/{id}/result`: **HTTP 200 JSON** ca hai mode. Plain = **strict version-1 ResultEnvelope** (`schemaVersion, data, artifacts, usage, warnings`). Encrypted = **strict version-1** `{schemaVersion, encrypted: true, delivery}` ; giai ma `delivery` ra **dung** ResultEnvelope do.
- `GET /api/v1/artifacts/{id}/download`: plain = **HTTP 200 raw bytes** + artifact MIME; encrypted = **HTTP 200 JSON** `{schemaVersion, encrypted: true, delivery, artifactId, mimeType}`. `RecipientDeliveryEnvelopeSchema` dung chung, **strict** cho ca hai.
- **302 bi lo khoi contract.** Chinh receipt ghi scope note: “docs/06-public-api.md still documents a 302 download” — tuc tai day moi la follow-up do chinh dispatch do dat ra, va no da duoc sua trong cycle nay.

**Mot luu y ve muc do bang chung: chua co independent receipt trong cay.** Mot lan verify doc lap **co chay** (coordinator log 3978: 64/64 = contracts 42/42 + delivery 22/22) nhung **khong append duoc** vao `tester.md` do byte 0x97 non-UTF8 lam hong file; `V-OFFLINE-RESULT-WIRE-01-REVAL` duoc re-dispatch nhung **van khong co** trong tester.md. Tien do doc lap co, **bieu bang chung** thi chua — D-A37-3. Con so **427** la full package, khong phai 427 test cua rieng wire; so 42/42 la targeted cua lan verify bi that.

**Sanh voi docs/06 cu**— va muc 12.30, day la **lan dau** mot muc trong hai file inventory nay ghi contract *da dong* chu khong phai *du kien chua trien khai*; cac muc 8.28/12.30 ghi ENC-08/ENC-09 van con `[ ]` va **khong** bi muc nay keo theo — freeze mot route khong dong task row nao.

Form gui `csrf`; verify constant-time; sai hoac thieu — **403** va applier khong chay (test assert store + audit rong); save POST-redirect-GET 302. Test goc nhat: lay token RA khoi HTML da render roi POST lai nhu trinh duyet.

**D112 DONG o code + offline** (khong socket that / browser / cookie do browser mint). **D110** webhook dispatcher van chua theo policy. **D113** cong CSRF hien kiem session cookie + secret, voi OIDC session store phai noi `verifySessionCsrf` — van mo. **D115** lane Qwen Admin da tu sua mot assertion hop dong cua Muc 26 (coi token CSRF la secret — sai theo thiet ke); day la thay doi **y nghia test** nen ai doc Muc 26 can biet. **D117** full sweep Muc 27 co 5 do, 2 do **moi khong phai cua lane do**; **khong suite crypto nao** trong danh sach do.

### W-CR28-01-ENCRYPTED-READ-PATH (2026-09-28)

- New module `services/orchestrator/src/modules/encryption/artifact-read-decrypt.ts` (sha256 `64b22116`, 10349 B) plus an S3 adapter in `server.ts`. The existing `CryptoStorageFacade` is hoisted and shared with the public upload gateway, and **both** read paths - the public download and the worker blob read - now decrypt before returning bytes, so **no read path returns ciphertext**. Restore was byte-exact: `server.ts` 191408 B sha `8d788f45`, test file 11519 B sha `f45bf7c0`.
- `services/orchestrator/tests/artifact-read-decrypt-offline.test.ts`: **14/14 tests passed**, exit **0**, on **three consecutive targeted runs**, plus a fourth run after the restore mutation. Counted from the file: **14 declared it/test blocks and zero `it.each`**, so the declared count is the executed count.
- Coverage read from the test file: plaintext passthrough; no manifest request for an unsealed object; successful decrypt; wrong key; wrong pinned object version; a missing pinned version refused rather than guessed; sidecar manifest pointer mismatch; a manifest missing a required field; a foreign artifact id; a foreign tenant refused as forbidden; no crypto configured; a missing object is 404; and the explicit property that ciphertext is never returned when decryption cannot succeed.
- Mutation M1 (disabling the `du-manifest-key` check) turns exactly the pointer-mismatch case red, so that branch is genuinely covered. **M2 was reported as not biting and was not counted**: removing the explicit `upload_token` guard still gave 14/14 because authentication fails closed at the AAD layer anyway, so the tests assert the **fail-closed outcome**, not the specific cause.
- `tsc --noEmit` exit **0** with an **empty log (0 bytes)**, re-run after restore.
- Independent receipt `T-CODEX-OFFLINE-CR28-01-INDEPENDENT` (Tester, 2026-09-28T13:57:33+07:00, read-only, changed no source or test file): 3 consecutive runs, each exit **0** with **1 passed suite, 14 passed tests**; orchestrator `tsc --noEmit` exit **0**. **No live S3, database, Vault or production download route was exercised.**
- Baseline before the change was `3 failed, 1 skipped, 96 passed, 99 of 100 total` suites / `3 failed, 28 skipped, 2133 passed, 2164 total` tests; the three red are the familiar unrelated admin-shell set.
- **Not accepted.** Δ66 is an **operational decision, not code**: the enable flag is **global, not per object**, so turning `publicUploadEncryption` off while sealed objects still exist makes the route fall back to `getBlob` and **serve raw ciphertext again** - the reader cannot tell from the flag whether an object is sealed. Δ67: no end-to-end test runs `GET /api/v1/artifacts/{id}/download` against a sealed object. Δ68: the `chunks` manifest branch has no test (needs >5 MiB). The finding own acceptance additionally asks for a real encrypted S3/PG fixture, a **restart** case, and a Tester live DB window; none of those exists.

### W-CR28-01-MANIFEST-CEILING (2026-09-29, `qwen-platform.md` Muc 34, Δ71)

- The packet names this item `W-PLAT-CR28-01-MANIFEST-CHUNKS-TEST`; **no receipt with that name exists** anywhere in the tree. The real item at Muc 34 is `W-CR28-01-MANIFEST-CEILING (Δ71)`, task_822866aa8d98.
- Finding, measured rather than assumed: **two different ceilings in two different files with two different error codes.** The crypto facade bounds by **chunk count** (`DEFAULT_MAX_CHUNKS = 65_536`, `validateManifest`, `INVALID_MANIFEST`), while `server.ts` bounds the sidecar manifest by **bytes** (8 MiB via `readStreamBounded`, `413 TOO_LARGE`). Measured manifest size is about **171 bytes per chunk**: ~49 000 chunks already reach **8.03 MiB**, and the facade default of 65 536 produces **10.74 MiB**.
- Consequence: for the **49 000–65 536 chunk** range the facade emits a manifest the server then refuses to read, and **no file reports the conflict** — the failure is "correct in one place, broken in another" rather than simply wrong. Four new tests in `crypto-storage-facade.test.ts` (**10 → 14**) pin the count ceiling and the contradiction. Three consecutive runs at **14/14** ExitCode **0/0/0**; `tsc` exit 0 with an **empty log**; production untouched (`crypto-storage-facade.ts` sha `dcd7e3db`, `server.ts` sha `22afb2ff`). Counted in the source: **14 declared, 0 `it.each`**.
- Δ71 still needs a coordinator decision between lowering the chunk default to about 48 000, raising the byte cap to at least 11 MiB, or deriving the byte cap from `maxChunks` so the two can never diverge. **Δ74 is new:** the byte cap lives in `server.ts`, outside the write scope of the lane that wrote these tests, so the `413` path has **no test guard at all**.

### W-CR28-01-METADATA-BOUNDARY-NEGATIVES (2026-09-29, `qwen-platform.md` Muc 37)

- The packet names this item `W-PLAT-CR28-04-METADATA-CRYPTO-BOUNDARY-TEST`; **no receipt with that name exists**, and the real item is filed under **CR28-01**, not CR28-04. It is `W-CR28-01-METADATA-BOUNDARY-NEGATIVES`, task_08997413d814. **8 negative tests** added, **zero production lines** touched: `server.ts` and every source file are unmodified, as the spec required.
- The eight: a `contextAad` that is not valid base64 is refused rather than leniently parsed; a `contextAad` swapped to another context is refused with `CONTEXT_MISMATCH` while the original still opens under its own binding, which proves the fixture is not simply broken; a mismatch on **each** of the three axes (slot / row / tenant); an invalid slot refused with `INVALID_INPUT` **before** the key provider is ever touched (asserted via `stats.wraps === 0`); empty `tenantId` or `refId` refused so nothing is sealed with an empty AAD; a wrong key on read failing closed without returning plaintext; and a provider that fails to unwrap surfacing as `KEY_PROVIDER_FAILED`.
- **A design finding the lane measured instead of assuming, and recorded as new Δ76.** `deriveAad` computes `sha256(``tenantId|slot|refId``, `utf8`)`, and JavaScript **substitutes** U+FFFD for invalid bytes, so two different byte strings can collapse to the same substituted string — in theory an AAD collision. The lane explicitly **declined** to claim it had proven the seam safe, and equally declined to write a vacuously green assertion of the form "outcome is either opened or refused" without saying what that proves. The test records that the outcome is one or the other but **never a silent success indistinguishable from a clean binding**. Whether to hard-block non-UTF-8 tenant ids is a **production change outside the write scope**; the proposal on record is to reject replacement code points, or to constrain tenant id to ASCII/UUID. Real-world exposure is judged low because a tenant id must still match through authentication before it reaches this seam.
- Mutation M1 **did bite, and was counted**: dropping `refId` from the AAD, i.e. losing the row axis, turned **5 tests red** — three from earlier cycles plus two new ones. The lane's reading is that 2 of the 8 new tests genuinely bite the row binding while the older ones confirm layered protection. Restore byte-exact: `metadata-crypto.ts` sha `aa200211`, 12138 B; `server.ts` and `runtime.ts` untouched.

- The packet names this item `W-ADM-UX-04-IDEMPOTENCY-NEGATIVE`; **no receipt with that name exists**. The real item is `W-ADMBASE-IDEMPOTENCY-NEGATIVE-HARDENING`, task_261984e0c6cd, filed under the ADM-BASE parent rather than ADM-UX-04. **No source code was changed** — only tests in `tests/admin-idempotency.test.ts` (**14 -> 27** tests, 3 -> 5 describes).
- **It read the file first and deliberately wrote no duplicates.** Already covered and left alone: 409 on a different payload, 409 on a different route, 422 for malformed keys (short, with a space, 201 chars), race rollback, misuse guard, purge completeness, and order-stable hashing. The 13 new tests target four real gaps:
- **Envelope** — the old tests only used `rejects.toMatchObject` on status and code and never checked the **real** envelope. The new tests go through `toProblem()`: 422 carries code `INVALID_SCHEMA`, type `urn:du:error:invalid_schema` and an echoed correlationId; 409 carries `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` with type `urn:du:error:idempotency_conflict`; and the serialised problem must contain neither the submitted key nor the payload value.
- **No duplicated side effect** — the old tests only asserted `counters.runs === 1`. The new tests assert the work does not run a second time **and** that the committed-world length is unchanged, for both the payload conflict and the route conflict.
- **Boundary** on the key pattern `/^[!-~]{8,200}$/` — 8 characters **pass** and 7 fail, 200 **pass** and 201 fail, with control and private-use characters all rejected. The stated reason matters: the old tests only checked the failing side and never the accepting side at the exact edge, so an **off-by-one in the regex would have slipped through**.
- **Honest replay** — a marker stored as 200 must replay as 200 rather than a fresh 201. The old tests only ever stored 201, so a regression changing the replayed status would not have been caught.
- **Expiry, mapped rather than invented** — the spec asks for an expired token, but the idempotency module has **no expiring token**; the only thing with an age is the marker TTL via the purge call. The lane mapped the requirement there and explicitly **refused to fabricate a fake token test**. Two cases: the purge honours its window parameter and returns the deleted count, and after expiry the key is **reusable** — the same key with a different payload runs again instead of a permanent 409. That second case is called the important one, because a purge that left a tombstone would lock a retrying client out of its own key forever.
- Evidence: **27/27 x3**; orchestrator typecheck Exit **0**; a 5-suite batch at **200/200 in three of four runs**. Counted in the source: **27 declared, 0 it.each**.
- **A transient red reported rather than hidden.** On one of the four batch runs, `admin-error-boundary-offline.test.ts` failed 2 tests. Investigated rather than assumed: run alone it is **22/22 green**, and the same batch re-run is **200/200 green**, twice more. That file binds a real loopback port and the audit suites bind too, so this is a **transient port collision** between suites binding at the same time and **not** caused by the change, which did not touch that file. The receipt calls it the **second** occurrence of this failure class after the earlier address-in-use flake, notes that ports were fixed for two audit suites but **not swept across the rest**, and records it as real unclosed technical debt needing its own packet to standardise port allocation for every loopback-binding suite.

### W-ADM-UX-03-TOOLBAR-BOUNDS-NEGATIVE (2026-09-29, `qwen-admin.md` Muc 43)

- The packet names this item `W-ADM-UX-05-AUDIT-TOOLBAR-NEGATIVE`; **no receipt with that name exists**. The real item is `W-ADM-UX-03-TOOLBAR-BOUNDS-NEGATIVE`, task_3bcbacb4bbd3. **No source code was changed** — only tests in `admin-audit-toolbar.test.ts` (**57 -> 67**, +10).
- The lane **surveyed first**: for each of the four spec cases it recorded what already existed (from > to, fractional limit, bad severity, over-long actor, all already covered at toolbar:109/161/243 and query:195/206/214) and what the real gap was — a one-sided window, a half-broken window, the **NaN** limit path, empty/whitespace/uppercase severity, and a long-but-**clean** token. It did not rewrite the four existing cases; the 10 new tests target only the gaps.
- **A wrong-payload finding it caught itself, and the reason it matters.** Its first test used a 64-character `a` string and went red. Assuming the 64 boundary was wrong, it wrote a probe scanning lengths 1..70 and found **ACCEPTED_MAX = 31, not 64**. The cause was not the regex: the token pattern allows up to 64 (verified in both src and dist), and what actually blocked the payload was the **solid-hex** rule — `a` is a valid hex character. So the 64-char `a` payload was rejected for the **solid-hex** reason, not for length. Had it simply bumped the number to make it green, it would have locked the **wrong rule** and the test would have protected nothing about the length boundary. It fixed the test by switching to a non-hex character `z`: 64 `z` accepted, 65 rejected **because of length**, so the 64/65 boundary now genuinely pins the length rule. The probe was deleted, not left in the file.
- The 10 new tests: a clean 65-character actor rejected with the **64 accept / 65 reject** boundary; fractional limit (NaN, `abc`, Infinity) falling to the contract default and never NaN; negative and decimal limit (-5 and 1.9) clamping to 1; both limit ends (1 accept, 200 accept, 201 -> 200); a one-sided window (only from, or only to) **accepted** rather than treated as missing; a half-broken window (valid from + broken to) **keeping from** and naming only `to`; empty or whitespace severity treated as **absent** rather than a recorded rejection; uppercase severity (WARNING) rejected **and named**, with no silent fold, re-pinning the earlier cycle; and a clean 300-character action rejected.
- Evidence: **67/67 x3**; orchestrator typecheck Exit **0**; regression across query, mount, route and idempotency at **76/76**. Counted in the source the file declares 67 tests. G-ADMIN-OPS stays **NO-GO**, ADM-UX-02 stays [~].
- **A transient red reported for the third time in this class** (cycles 36, 42, 43). On one regression run `admin-audit-query.test.ts` failed 1 test; run alone it is **26/26 green** and the same 4-suite batch re-runs **76/76 green**. Cause is a **transient loopback port collision** between concurrently binding suites, not the change. The receipt states plainly that this is no longer a sporadic matter: every parallel run carries the risk of a false red, and standardising port allocation for **every** loopback-binding suite needs its own packet.
### W-CR28-01-FACADE-BOUNDARY-NEGATIVES (2026-09-29, `qwen-platform.md` Muc 38)

- The packet names this item `W-PLAT-CR28-01-STORAGE-FACADE-CRYPTO-BOUNDS`; **no receipt with that name exists**. The real item is `W-CR28-01-FACADE-BOUNDARY-NEGATIVES`, task_be3bf21a6279. **9 negative tests** added to `tests/crypto-storage-facade.test.ts` (**18 -> 27**), **0 production lines**: `server.ts` and every source file untouched, per spec.
- The nine, in four groups. **Chunk-count boundary**: a manifest with exactly `maxChunks` is accepted and one below is rejected (two-sided), and a nonsense `maxChunks` at construction (0, -1, 65537, 1.5, NaN) is refused rather than trusted. **Invalid sidecar format**: a field **outside the allow-list** is refused, `chunks.length` differing from `totalChunks` is refused, and a **duplicate nonce** across chunks is refused because nonce reuse is a GCM catastrophe. **Non-JSON sidecar**: a string, null, number, array, bool or undefined is refused. **Missing envelope header**: refusal when **each** field is omitted in turn (version, algorithm, chunkSizeBytes, totalSizeBytes, fileSha256, contextAad, chunks, dek, manifestMac), plus a wrong version or wrong algorithm.
- **Scope measured, not assumed.** Fetching the sidecar bytes and `JSON.parse` happen in the S3 adapter inside `server.ts`, outside the write scope. What the facade **owns** — and what these tests pin — is the second half: given a value that came out of a sidecar, does the facade **refuse** it. A non-record (a shape `JSON.parse` would let through, or throw on) must be refused rather than throwing a TypeError from `isRecord`.
- **Its mutation was invalid and it said so rather than counting it.** Disabling the manifest allow-list check (`if (false && Object.keys...)`) did **not compile** (0 tests ran) because it becomes dead code that TypeScript catches. That is a void probe and is **not** counted as evidence. Of the nine, the **envelope-header** (8/9) and **chunk-count** (1/2/3) groups carry the most weight because they push real data through the facade; the allow-list / nonce / non-JSON group checks input shape and the lane could **not** find a mutation that both compiles and changes behaviour. It recorded this so nobody assumes all nine were mutation-verified.
- Verify: `crypto-storage-facade` three consecutive runs at **27/27** ExitCode **0/0/0**; `tsc` exit 0 with an **empty log**; test file sha `120a4aa9` / 30204 B; `crypto-storage-facade.ts` **unchanged** at sha `dcd7e3db` / 31593 B; `server.ts` untouched at sha `22afb2ff`. Δ74, Δ71, Δ75, Δ69, Δ66 and Δ76 are all unchanged; **Δ71 (byte-vs-count) remains the only delta with a production consequence and still has no decision**. The sidecar byte-read and `JSON.parse` stay in `server.ts` and remain untestable from this file — the same limit noted in cycle 36.

- **The file is the hard one, and the receipt explains why it is cheaper than it looks**: it has 13 call sites of `createAdminShellServer`, but all 13 funnel through a single wrapper, so fixing the wrapper shrinks the change from 13 places to 1. Binding happens inside `handle.listen()` *after* the factory returns, which is exactly why the retry loop is wrapped around `listen()` and every call site stays untouched.
- **Before**: `QUIET_PORT_BASE = 44600 + (pid % 10) * 16` gives only **10 buckets**, so two processes sharing `pid % 10` pick the same band and collide; the wrapper passed a fixed port with **no retry**, so one occupied port turned the whole suite red. The file is **CRLF** while the two audit files are LF, so any edit had to match EOL.
- **After**: a fresh **43000-43504** band, deliberately disjoint from the 42000-42504 band the two audit suites use, so all three can run in parallel. Retry is bounded and **swallows only `EADDRINUSE`**, rethrowing anything else. The unguarded `afterAll` at line 201 was guarded for consistency with the other seven blocks.
- **Verified against source, not just the receipt**: the wrapper does gate on `err.code !== "EADDRINUSE" || attempt >= PORT_BAND_SLOTS` and the comments state the disjointness is **hygiene, not the guarantee** — the bounded retry is the guarantee. The `afterAll` at line 201 now reads `if (shell) await shell.handle.close()`. The file is still CRLF (1472 CRLF) after the edit.
- **Evidence is concurrent, which is the condition that used to flake**: platform-mount three times sequential **37/37**; **2 platform-mount + 1 audit-mount + 1 audit-query at the same time 4/4 green**, zero port collisions; a 9-suite audit+shell batch **447/447**, 9/9 suites; `tsc --noEmit` ExitCode 0.
- **Counted from source**: **37 `it()` + 3 `test()`, 0 `it.each`** — the executed total matches the receipt 37 exactly, with no `skip`/`todo`/`only`.
- **Result**: the `EADDRINUSE` debt in the admin area is considered **closed for these three files**. Gates unchanged: `G-ADMIN-OPS` **NO-GO**, `ADM-UX-02` **[~]**, `G-ENC` / `G6` **NO-GO**.

### W-ADM-UX-02-IDEMPOTENCY-NEGATIVE (2026-09-29, `qwen-admin.md` Muc 48)

- **Scope**: only `tests/admin-idempotency.test.ts`; no production code. 27 -> **45 tests**. `task_4d91a27e8c3b` / `ctx_4d91a27e8c3b`.
- **The method is the notable part: the author probed real behaviour before writing tests** rather than guessing and then adjusting to fit. Two probes on the real function settled assertions that would otherwise have been assumptions, including that `hash(null)`, `hash(undefined)` and `hash({})` are **identical** while `hash("")` and `hash([])` each differ from the empty object.
- **The Unicode case is the sharpest.** A combining acute accent is **2 code units but 1 grapheme**; if the bound check normalized before measuring, a key built from combining marks would collapse into the 8..200 range and be **accepted**, meaning the canonical form of a credential would depend on Unicode normalization. Both bounds are pinned: 8 characters + 1 combining mark, and 100 combining marks = **200 code units**, both must 422, alongside 8 / 200 / 201.
- **Null and empty hash, six tests**, locking the distinctions the probe found: inside an object, `undefined` is dropped by JSON so `{a: undefined}` equals `{}` while `{a: null}` does not. The receipt is candid that this test is only correct **because it was measured, not assumed**.
- **Purge race**: a marker is purged and the same key is then reused with a **different** payload, which must run fresh. The stated reason matters — if purge left a tombstone, a client retrying after the window would be permanently 409-ed by its own key. A second test asserts purging an empty store returns 0 rather than erroring.
- **A deliberately recorded product behaviour**: the route does **not** validate the shape of a stored body, so a string where an object belongs is replayed verbatim, and a null body is replayed with status 500. The author explicitly notes that **idempotency still holds** — `runs = 0` in both cases, no repeated side effect — but that a corrupt body reaches the pane with no shape check. Recorded, not fixed, because it is out of scope.
- **Verification**: `admin-idempotency.test.ts` three times sequential, **45/45** each; `tsc --noEmit` Exit Code 0; **regression across 7 related suites 277/277**, 7/7 suites.
- **Counted from source**: **45 `it()`, 0 `it.each`** — matches the receipt exactly.
- **Result**: `G-ADMIN-OPS` **NO-GO**, `ADM-UX-02` **[~]**, `G-ENC` / `G6` **NO-GO**. Offline only, no commit/push.

### W-PLAT-CR28-05-CRYPTO-STORAGE-FACADE-NEGATIVE (2026-09-29, `qwen-platform.md` Muc 45)

- **A real `[PASS]` with two mutations, and the packet came from the inbox** rather than a file on disk (`msg_84676fec5d9d`, sequence 283) — recorded because it is a difference in how the evidence was delivered, not in its strength.
- **Deliverable +74 tests (27 -> 101)**, and **0 lines of production code**: `crypto-storage-facade.ts` stays `dcd7e3db`, which the receipt notes **matches the sha recorded at Mục 36/37**, so there is no drift.
- **Coverage by group**: context AAD bounds, including the gap that led the deliverable — **`purpose` was in the AAD but no test had ever varied it**; malformed sidecar fields (9 wrong-type top-level, 9 wrong-type inside a chunk, a `totalSizeBytes` that disagrees with the chunk sum, and a correctly-shaped MAC with the wrong bytes, which yields `AUTHENTICATION_FAILED` and proves the MAC is a real check); stream chunk boundaries; and metadata tampering.
- **Error-code split is a large part of what was pinned**: version/algorithm wrong on a single-shot object gives `INVALID_INPUT`, on a sidecar gives `INVALID_MANIFEST`, and a damaged tag gives `INVALID_MANIFEST` — three code paths for one class of metadata problem. Several cases are recorded as findings rather than expectations.
- **Verification**: three consecutive runs, each **101 passed, 101 total**. `tsc --noEmit` Exit Code 0 with a **0-byte log**, before M1 and after restore. Test file sha `29206e36` / 49096 B, and the file is **CRLF** — verified from source as 976 CRLF with 0 bare LF.
- **Both mutations went red on NEW tests, which is the strongest form of this evidence.** M1a removes `purpose` from `contextAad`: **2 failed, 99 passed**, and the two red are new tests. That is the proof `purpose` is genuinely inside the AAD rather than a decorative field — before this cycle, removing it turned **nothing** red. M1b changes `decodeBase64` from `invalidManifest` to `invalidInput`: **4 failed, 97 passed**, all four new, which is what makes Δ97 a demonstrated state rather than the author's conjecture. Both restores byte-exact, then 101/101 and a 0-byte tsc log.
- **A wrong hypothesis, discarded before any test was written — worth recording.** The author suspected `manifestMac` was decorative, because `validateManifest` only shape-checks it. Reading further into `decryptChunkGenerator` showed the MAC **is** genuinely verified: it computes an HMAC from the manifest MAC key and runs `timingSafeEqual` **before decrypting any chunk**. The hypothesis was dropped, and a test was then written that **refutes** it — substituting a different 32-byte base64 MAC of the same length and alphabet yields `AUTHENTICATION_FAILED`. The receipt explicitly names the lesson: read the whole path before concluding a guard is decorative, the same class of error as `err.detail` at Mục 42.
- **An EOL guard prevented a mixed-EOL file**: the test file is CRLF while the author's chunk was written with LF, which would have produced a mixed-EOL file. The guard threw first; the join was fixed to normalise CRLF and the result pinned with `bareLF: 0`. The receipt notes the three test files edited across these cycles have different EOL (LF, LF, CRLF) and that the rule is **read the file, do not guess** — the same rule this lane applies.
- **Counted from source**: **34 `it()` + 12 `it.each` blocks + 10 `test()`**, 0 `test.failing`, 0 `skip`. The declared and executed totals differ because of the table-driven blocks.
- **Five new findings, none closed** — see the delta list below. The clearest is that the **stream error code leaks into the single-shot API**: `decodeBase64` calls `invalidManifest()`, so `decrypt()` on an object with no manifest and no chunks still returns `INVALID_MANIFEST` when the tag is missing or malformed. Every caller switching on the code must handle a code that does not apply to the path it called.

### W-PLAT-CR28-06-SUBMISSION-METADATA-CRYPTO-NEGATIVE (2026-09-29, `qwen-platform.md` Muc 46)

- **A real `[PASS]`**: 42/42 ×3 with a genuine `PASS` line, and a mutation that turned **8** tests red.
- **Deliverable +35 tests (7 -> 42)**, and **0 production lines**: `submission.ts` `e25670af` / 32535 B matches the values recorded at Mục 26/32, and `metadata-crypto.ts` `aa200211` matches Mục 36/37/44. Test `438744e6` / 29018 B, and the file is **LF** — the receipt notes this is the opposite of the Mục 45 file and that the EOL guard checks on every write. Confirmed from source: 0 CRLF, 663 bare LF.
- **Coverage**: cross-column swapping in **both** directions — the reverse direction is the addition over the earlier cycle, which only had the forward one — plus a right-slot/wrong-`refId` case that separates the two binding dimensions, both columns under a different tenant, and two columns carrying genuinely different envelopes. Malformed envelope work pins a **specific error code per variant** rather than asserting only that something is rejected.
- **The new evidence that matters most: atomic seal before the transaction.** Source at `submission.ts:253-256` shows both seal calls run **before** `db.tx` opens, and the tests prove it rather than only reading it: the provider counts its calls and throws on the Nth. With the first wrap failing, one call and **zero** writes; with the second failing, two calls and still **zero** writes.
- **The author explains why that counter is the load-bearing part of the test**: if the code only wrapped once, a fail-on-2 provider would never fire and the test would be **green while proving nothing**. This is the anti-vacuity property stated out loud, and it is the same discipline that produced the control test in the admin receipt this cycle.
- **M1** simulates submit sealing only one column by skipping the payload column: **8 failed, 34 passed** — 2 pre-existing and **6 new**. Notably the test *a Vault outage on the second wrap writes nothing* goes **red** because `wrapCalls()` is only 1, meaning **the anti-vacuity assertion caught the mutation itself**. Restored byte-exact, then 42/42 and a 0-byte typecheck log. - **Counted from source**: `submission-metadata-crypto-e2e` declares **19 `it()` + 7 `it.each` blocks** = **42 executed**, matching the receipt. 0 `test.failing`, 0 `skip`.
- **Three new findings, none closed** — see the delta list below. The clearest is an **asymmetry between write and read**: the seal path does not validate the shape of the wrapped DEK the provider returns, so a misbehaving provider makes submit **succeed** and write an envelope that can never be opened, while the read path has a validator that simply is not called on the write side.

### W-ADM-UX-09-BUSINESS-VIEW-MODEL-NEGATIVE (2026-09-29, `qwen-admin.md` Muc 53)

- **Scope**: only `tests/admin-business-view-model.test.ts`; no production code. 54 -> **95 tests (+41)**. `task_5c91a4038e4a` / `ctx_5c91a4038e4a`.
- **Probed the real function before writing assertions again, and the suite was green on the first run** — the receipt states no assertion was revised afterwards, which is the outcome the probe-then-assert method is supposed to produce.
- **Four measured behaviours, several of them surprising**: a broken business status still **degrades** (a `default` branch returns a neutral badge and the raw label) rather than throwing, but `resolveVersionHealth` with an unknown status returns **healthy**; HTML in a business id or version is passed straight into the label and into interpolated `title`/`message`/`summary` strings; heartbeat boundaries are 60s online, 61s degraded, 300s degraded, 301s offline, a future timestamp reads **online**, `nowMs = NaN` reads **offline**, and garbage reads `none`; and with two rows both marked active, a **RETIRED** row beats the genuinely ENABLED one.
- **Four defects recorded, not fixed** (outside scope). The most serious: an unknown status reads as **healthy**, because the health function excludes RETIRED, DRAINING and REGISTERED_DISABLED and then **defaults everything else to ENABLED** — a row can look green while being non-actionable, which the receipt calls a more dangerous state than showing red. Also: the health counts do not reconcile with the total (`totalVersions = 1` while the four counters sum to zero), a malformed heartbeat is swallowed into `status: none` with a nulled timestamp so the breakage becomes invisible, and a rolled-over date like `2026-02-30` is passed back to the renderer verbatim.
- **A control test that prevents the defect table going green meaninglessly** — the part of this receipt I would point a reader to first. The badge path does **not** throw, because a `default` returns neutral, so applying another lane's inference that a broken enum throws would be wrong here. The author wrote a dedicated control asserting all four real states take their own branch, so the broken-state rows above cannot pass vacuously. The one place the module does throw is `buildVersionTransitionConfirm` on an unknown action.
- **Verification**: `admin-business-view-model.test.ts` three consecutive times, **95/95**, exit 0/0/0; baseline at HEAD measured by **running** the HEAD version at **54 tests**, so the +41 is a measured difference rather than an asserted one; `tsc --noEmit` Exit Code 0; **regression across 5 view-model and list-contract suites 465/465**, 5/5.
- **Counted from source**: **71 `test()`**, 0 `it.each`, 0 `test.failing`, 0 `skip` — but the file also contains nested `describe` blocks, so **71 is the declaration count and 95 the executed count**. The file is **CRLF** (912 CRLF, 0 bare LF).
- **Result**: `G-ADMIN-OPS` **NO-GO**, `ADM-UX-09` **[~]**, `G-SEC` / `G-ENC` / `G6` **NO-GO**. Offline only, no commit/push.

### W-ADM-UX-11-OVERVIEW-VIEW-MODEL-NEGATIVE (2026-09-29, `qwen-admin.md` Muc 55)

- **Scope**: only `tests/admin-overview-view-model.test.ts`; no production code. 49 -> **108 tests (+59)**. `task_7c91a4038e6c` / `ctx_7c91a4038e6c`.
- **The packet asked for corrupt *throughput* metrics, and the module has none.** The author counted the exports of `overview-view-models.ts` — exactly ten functions, **none of which computes a rate or throughput**; usage is projected as **raw counters**. Rather than invent a throughput concept to satisfy the packet, they wrote that down and tested the corrupt-counter cases (NaN, negative, infinity, negative cost) properly instead. That is the second time in two cycles a lane has declined to fabricate a concept the packet named and recorded the absence instead.
- **One module, three different degradation modes for the same bad enum — the section the author flags as their most memorable finding, and the reason not to carry inference across lanes:**
  - `usageMeasurementBadge` / `Label` — unguarded table lookup ⇒ **throws TypeError**.
  - `auditKindLabel` / `auditKindSeverity` — unguarded table lookup ⇒ **throws TypeError**.
  - `auditSeverityBadge` — a `switch` with **no default** ⇒ **returns undefined silently**.
  - `buildHealthOverviewView` — a binary ternary ⇒ reports **degraded**, the safe direction.
- **The third row is the one to keep.** Its return type is declared as a four-member union that **does not include undefined**, so TypeScript cannot catch it: a `switch` covering all four union members is treated as exhaustive, and at runtime it falls through to nothing. The author attached a **control** to the first two rows so those lines cannot go green meaninglessly — the same discipline recorded in the previous cycle, now applied again in a third file.

- **Five defects recorded, not fixed**, and the first is a silent downgrade: `buildAuditEventView` **never reads** `row.severity`, inferring it from `kind`, so a row declaring `severity: error` with `kind: operation.cancel` comes out as `warning` and the original value **disappears from the view**.
- **The second is tenant scoping collapsing to a no-op**: the filter is a strict equality, so when **both** sides are `undefined` the comparison is **true** and a view with no tenant keeps every tenant-less event. The docstring reasoning is correct for present values and **wrong for absent ones**, and the author has a test creating two different tenants both missing the id and both landing in one view. The comparison is also case-sensitive, so a view for `T1` keeps no `t1` events.
- **The third**: `totals` is carried **by reference**, not copied, so mutating it after projection changes the displayed value. It is also never reconciled against the row sum — rows summing to 7 operations while totals says 0, and nobody asks.
- **The fourth**: the time window is neither parsed nor ordered. Reversed windows, garbage strings, empty strings, zero-length windows, mixed epoch-milliseconds-versus-ISO, and a rolled-over `2026-02-30` all reach the renderer intact.
- **The fifth**: `fullyHealthy` is not guaranteed boolean, because it is an and-chain — two false **strings** return the string, and a database field of `false` renders as healthy. The author notes this is the same truthiness class as a `hasValue` defect in an earlier cycle.
- **Beyond the five**, also recorded: every usage counter is an unclamped passthrough (NaN, negative, infinity, negative zero, negative cost, beyond the safe-integer limit); `allUnattributed` requires **both** provider and model to be the unattributed marker, so a row attributed on one side only still counts as attributed and the empty-state copy does not show; null events and a null events array both throw; and `message` and `actor` are passed through without escaping.
- **A self-correction worth recording**: three of the author own tests failed because they assumed a helper default — the base audit row defaults to a different tenant, so every event was filtered out and the test read index 0 of an empty array. They identified it as their error rather than a product bug, fixed it by passing the tenant explicitly, and **left a comment in the test** so the next person does not step on it again.
- **Verification**: `admin-overview-view-model.test.ts` three consecutive times, **108/108**, exit 0/0/0; baseline at HEAD measured by **running** the HEAD version at **49 tests**, so +59 is measured rather than asserted; `tsc --noEmit` Exit Code 0; **regression across all 7 admin view-model suites plus 2 contract suites: 687/687**, 9/9.
- **Counted from source**: **71 test()** against **108 executed**; the file contains a loop over the full kind list at L550, which is where the extra executions come from. 0 `test.failing`, 0 `skip`. File is CRLF (943 CRLF, 0 bare LF).
- **Result**: `G-ADMIN-OPS` **NO-GO**, `ADM-UX-11` **[~]**, `G-SEC` / `G-ENC` / `G6` **NO-GO**. Offline only, no commit/push.

### W-PLAT-CR28-07-ADMIN-CRYPTO-CONFIG-NEGATIVE (2026-09-29, `qwen-platform.md` Muc 47)

- **A real `[PASS]` with two mutations**: 83/83 ×3 with a genuine `PASS` line, **+59 tests (24 -> 83)**, and **0 production lines** in the delivered state. The two production files touched temporarily for the mutations were restored **byte-identical** (`crypto-config-api.ts` `94c71214`, `crypto-config-view-models.ts` `707f742a`); `server.ts` `22afb2ff` was never touched. Test `fa88ed10` / 39994 B / LF.
- **M1a** loosens the fingerprint preview from 12 to 32 characters: **6 failed, 77 passed** — 2 pre-existing and **4 new** (the 12/13 boundary, the `md5:` no-prefix case, and the 12-leading-characters finding). The receipt notes this is a *larger leak* and it was caught immediately. **M1b** loosens the `assertTenantId` regex: **12 failed, 71 passed**, and **all twelve are new tests**. Both restores byte-identical, then 83/83 and a 0-byte typecheck log.
- **M1b comes with an attribution detail worth keeping**: the empty-string variant stays green because the mutated expression still requires at least one character, and the author says so explicitly — isolating that this is a property of the mutation, not of every malformed id. That is the difference between "twelve tests went red" and "twelve tests went red for this reason".
- **Four new findings, none closed** — see the delta list below. The sharpest is a **CSRF gate that disappears when a claim is absent**: the write-auth check returns early on an undefined cookie role, including for a tenant-operator principal, so it tests for the *presence of a verified cookie claim* rather than the *kind of credential*.
- **The author is explicit that this cannot be exploited today**: a cross-site page still cannot supply the authorization header a bearer principal requires. The risk is conditional — if the server ever accepts a principal from another source, the CSRF gate would vanish silently — and it is written up as such rather than inflated.
- **Four self-corrections, all surfaced by running**: a positive control used the wrong instrument (asserting a raw NUL appears in serialised output, when the serialiser escapes control characters, so the test was red **for the wrong reason**); an assertion predicting two different 403 wordings when measurement showed the viewer check runs first and both messages are identical, which the author calls **better** behaviour and inverted the assertion accordingly; an off-by-one in a character count corrected and then pinned with a length assertion so the number is machine-checked rather than eyeballed; and a quoting bug fixed by building the string from character codes — the very two characters the escaping function handles.

### The part of Muc 47 I would point a reader to first — two PRE-EXISTING leak tests that pass for weaker reasons than they appear

- The author applied the rule that a negative assertion is worth only what it actually demonstrates, to **tests that were already in the suite**:
  - One test asserting the view model and audit rows carry no key material bans the sentinel in the serialised view — but the sentinel is banned from the **public key field**, not from the **fingerprint field**. It passes even when the fingerprint field carries that exact sentinel. The author wrote a test demonstrating this.
  - A second test asserting a fingerprint carrying a secret shows only its preview does ban a sentinel **already inside** the fingerprint — but it only checks that the **whole string** is absent. Measured, **the first 12 characters of it are present**.
- So both leak tests in the suite were green for a reason weaker than their appearance. The author **kept them** and added tests that measure exactly which characters reach the output, with a positive control where the sentinel sits in the field actually under test.
- **This is the failure mode that never warns you**: a test that passes for the wrong reason gives no signal at all. It is worth recording next to the three cycles of `test.failing` blocks, because it is the same underlying problem — a green result whose strength was never checked — arriving from the opposite direction.

### A limit stated rather than faked

- The packet asked for **CSRF timing boundaries**. The author states plainly that **an offline unit test cannot demonstrate a constant-time property**, and that writing a nicely-named timing test anyway would not be honest. Instead they pinned what **is** observable: the decision boundary (64 is the only valid length; 63/65/128/129 are all refused) and the fact that the length comparison happens **before** the constant-time comparison.
- Closing the timing question for real needs a **live sentinel** at the route layer — named as Tester lane work with a real window. That is the third time in this run of a lane declining to manufacture a test the evidence cannot support.
